  filed: 2026-09-05 Frank
  status (2026-09-05): not started; CHARTERED by Frank 2026-09-05, fifty-fifth session, UNSCHEDULED: "changing the code usage of ascii characters from numbers to char literals if that works or embedded comments if it doesn't. only for code reading for those of us that can't convert numbers to ascii in our heads"
  trigger: [VM-LEN-HOIST]'s build (item 3 lands there; the rest ride the same abi event if cheap), or a VM-readability pass at the 1.0 doc tier.
  refs: decisions D76 D94; known_issues K35
  Today an emitted compare reads `subject[scan_position] == 58`; the row renders printable-ASCII byte values as char literals (`== ':'`) and non-printable/non-ASCII values as the number plus a fixed-form comment (`== 206 /* 0xCE, UTF-8 lead */` — exact comment vocabulary to be designed; UTF-8 lead/continuation bytes should say what they are, control bytes name their escape). SITES: the emitted compare/range tests (both emitters), table initializers where a legend already exists (the [M6-READ] state legends may already discharge tables — survey first), the class-test range arithmetic (`(unsigned)(subject[p] - 177) <= 14u` wants its range endpoints named in a comment since char literals cannot express arithmetic offsets).
  RITUAL, all pre-priced by precedent: [M6-READ] is the model (approved-sample-first, then conversion); char literals are compile-time identical so run_object_neutrality.sh proves `.text`/`.rodata` byte-identity for free; the SOURCE bytes move on every artifact → a D76/D94 abi event (bump + re-pin (B) + grep for EVERY reader of emitted text — the manifest/size-log/anchor re-records exactly like this morning's battery repair slice; run the [SABANCHOR] tripwire before delivering); comment-form additions move comment-INCLUDED counts (RX_VM_PROGRAM_BYTES) but not comment-excluded code bytes — size-cap semantics unchanged, say so in the spec hunk; char literals shift code bytes by ±1/value — census the delta, expect noise.
  ONE RENDERER: a single emit-side helper (value → rendered token + optional comment) used by every site, never per-site formatting (memory: general mechanisms; and a K35-style check that the helper is the only spelling). Sonnet-sized with an approved sample first per [M6-READ]; unscheduled, fits any quiet slot after the current utf8 rows settle. ABSORBED [VM-READABLE] (was STATE:not-started; merged 2026-10-12, plclean [PLAN-LINT] step 2) NOTE: VM-READABLE item (3) spells bytes as numbers (`!= 123`) where READ-BYTES' rule would render them as characters (`'{'`) — settle that when the pair is designed; one emitted-readability change, ONE abi event (the [M6-READ] pass is the model).: (BOONIES TIER — Frank, 2026-10-12 session 110, filed UNSCHEDULED: "vm readability") — make the emitted VM body read as straight-line code.
  Frank's items: (1) a label macro `#define LABEL(XX) XX: __attribute__((unused))`; (2) labels need no per-artifact prefix — they are function-local, so a plain `rx_` (e.g. `rx_L7`) in every artifact; (3) the line shape `if (scan_position + minimum_required_length_from_here > subject_length) goto rx_fail;` once at a stretch's entry ([VM-LEN-HOIST]'s check), then per byte `if (subject[scan_position] != 123) goto rx_fail; scan_position++; goto rx_L7;` (fail-first, no `scan_position < subject_length` per byte); (4) elide a `goto` whose target label is the very next statement; (5) elide a label nothing jumps to.
  CONSTRAINTS: changes emitted scaffolding = an abi event + identity-gate re-pin (D76/D94, readers by grep); the `rx_L<N>` labels are shared vocabulary with `--emit-ir` (M6-READ: renaming them breaks the listing's correspondence — keep the names the listing prints); object-code neutrality is the acceptance for items (1), (2), (4), (5) (executed bytes + exported symbols, M6-READ's `check_neutrality.sh` definition), item (3) is [VM-LEN-HOIST]'s measured change and rides it. TRIGGER: [VM-LEN-HOIST]'s build (item 3 lands there; the rest ride the same abi event if cheap), or a VM-readability pass at the 1.0 doc tier.

- [EMIT-ENTRIES] STATE:not-started — ENTRY-POINT EMIT CONTROL
  filed: 2026-09-21 Frank
  status (2026-10-17): not started; CHARTERED by Frank 2026-09-21; 2026-10-17 (D163 add. 12): E1 keeps every export an independent controller over static parts, so this row can drop any entry (e.g. one of `_search`/`_search_in`) without re-layout; the formal `--emit` language (entries, main, comments) is this row's
  next: SCHEDULED (Frank 2026-10-23, "Yes"): starts right after I1+I2 land on main, design note first (unit names by behaviour per D174, the .rxt selection attribute, the emitted-set stamp); its own row, not deferred to [IFACE-PUSH]; motive: the iterators' size finding (review 2026-10-23-r2)
  refs: docs/spec/match_api.md; src/gen/entries.c; docs/dev/lanes/cpb02_report.md; decisions D18 D26 D76 D77 D88 D104 D118
  Today every artifact exports the five `<prefix>`-scoped entries unconditionally (docs/spec/match_api.md §3¶1; `--no-captures` is the only subtractive lever and it selects an engine shape, not an entry set). The row: a caller names the entry set it wants (a flag, a `.rxt` `target` attribute, or both — the manager designs, Frank rules); an entry not named is emitted `static` when another named entry depends on it (the un-suffixed entries forward to the `_in` twins, match_api.md §6.3.8¶11 — so naming `_search` alone makes `_search_in` static and drops `_match*` entirely), and not emitted at all when nothing named needs it. What it buys: smaller artifacts and no exported symbol a consumer did not ask for (the D104 hazard class).
  Contract: the `rx_info.nentries`/`search_form` stamps already exist to describe the set — the spec hunk says what a reduced set stamps. abi event by construction (D76). Design gate before code (the four lenses, memory `pcrec-design-evaluation-lenses`): is the entry set a per-target attribute (source-file world, D118) or a per-invocation flag, and how the harness's identity gates treat a reduced artifact.
  ABSORBED [EMIT-SET] (was STATE:not-started; merged 2026-10-12, plclean [PLAN-LINT] step 2) NOTE: B0 of [COMPOUND] already built the entry-table internals (src/gen/entries.c, `pcrec_entry_linkage`; docs/dev/lanes/cpb02_report.md "B0 [EMIT-ENTRIES] internal") — this row is the caller-facing control over which entries/units are emitted.: (D88 2026-08-29: ONE artifact per emitted file, always — an emission set is a DIRECTORY of per-artifact files + a manifest header, composition is LINKING; no multi-pattern TU; R-VE-3's cross-artifact table sharing is link-level, deferred D77) — CONTROL OF WHAT IS EMITTED INTO THE C FILE (Frank, 2026-08-24, thirty-ninth session): a caller who only uses `match` does not need `search`; one who never reflects does not need `rx_info`; `main` folds into the same switch — `--emit -search,-rx_info,main` (a comma list of named EMISSION UNITS, `-` removes, bare name adds, defaults = today's artifact).
  The point beyond trimming: once emission is a SET of named units, VALUE-ADD units become drop-ins on the same switch — the results → static-struct copy helper ([V-I]), the compiled search/replace code ([M4-SUBST]), rx code-management helpers (versioning/identity stamps a consumer can query), the diagnostic variants ([V-H] `--trace`/[ENG-PGO] `--profile` are the same shape: a separate emitted variant selected at generation time), the caller-provided-buffer `_in` entries ([DD-14.FB] — a unit a caller may drop), a [LIB] library's shared regions.
  Design points at charter: the unit graph (dependencies — `main` needs `search`; `_in` needs the run struct; `rx_info` fields some units reference) resolved at generation with a clean refusal for an inconsistent set (D26 tier 3 wording); the [ABI-NS]/structural codegen checks become per-unit (a unit's presence/absence is a byte-level fact the identity gates assert — the default set byte-identical to today's artifact, the control); `--list-emit` prints the units and their defaults; the CLI/library API surface (`pcrec_options` gains the set; DD-3 versioning). D18's principle applied to the artifact's SURFACE: what is not emitted costs nothing, and a unit nobody asked for is not there to be wrong. Relations: `--emit-main` (today's only switch, becomes the `main` unit), OS-0's named-entry-point discipline, [DD-8] (`--emit-ir`/`--emit-dot` are OUTPUT formats, not units — keep them apart).
  Not started unprompted; PLANNED, its spine status deliberately open (Frank, 2026-08-24 ~22:0x) — the going-forward conversation at [DD-14]'s close decides.

- [CAP-EARLY-STOP] STATE:not-started — stop the capture walk at the last group's close when the span is already proven
  filed: 2026-10-09 Frank
  status (2026-10-09): not started; FILED 2026-10-09 by Frank, session 102: "if the match is done, the capture walk can end at the close of the last [group]. this is a general statement"; FOR EVALUATION
  trigger: a hybrid or stage-2 cell where the VM's span walk is a measured share.
  refs: decisions D156; member of [CAP-SPAN] (2026-10-26)
  when the match span is already PROVEN (the hybrid's exact window, REVEND stage 2, any SPAN-handing locator), the VM runs only to place capture groups, so it can stop at the last capturing group's close instead of walking to the match end. CORRECTNESS CONDITION (manager): a group's boundaries depend on whether the REMAINDER can still match (e.g. `(a*)ab` on `aaab`: group 1 is `aa` because `ab` must follow), so stopping at the last close p needs "the remainder after the last group matches exactly [p, end)". A reverse DFA of that remainder, walked once back from the proven end, marks every viable p; the VM stops at the last close the moment its position is marked. GENERAL: applies to every VM-over-proven-span finisher (D156 FINISH's VM hat), not only REVEND.
  EVAL: the remainder machine's size (the pattern suffix after the last close), patterns whose last close sits inside a loop (the last close is not static: the condition is the last close of the LAST ITERATION, which may need the mark at every close), the give-up surface (it can only shorten VM work: one-way or neutral), and the population: capture patterns with a long uncaptured tail (`(\w+)@[a-z.]+`, `(\d+)\.txt$`, log lines with a leading capture). Trigger: a hybrid or stage-2 cell where the VM's span walk is a measured share.

- [FIND-MIX] STATE:not-started — WEIGHTED MIXTURE of findings data
  filed: 2026-09-28 Frank
  status (2026-09-28): not started; FILED by Frank 2026-09-28, eighty-fourth session, D125: filed, UNSCHEDULED; pairs with [FIND-UTF8-DEFAULT]
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: decisions D77 D125
  Witness: JSON subjects carrying Russian strings. json-only leaves Cyrillic at the zero floor; russian-only ranks JSON structure (" : {) as RARE — worse than no data; today's `include` composes PER KIND by shadowing (first block along the chain answers), never by addition, so json + a russian cpfreq answers every -e utf8 question from the russian table alone. The right composition: rate = w·A + (1−w)·B, w = the subject's share of each source; w only matters on keys BOTH sources carry. Counts are the stored form (lossless, additive merge — design §0.1/R3-R4), so it is scale-then-add. BUILD ORDER: analyzer-side first (`pcrec-analyze --merge A:w B:(1−w)` emitting an ordinary bundle; no compiler change); an in-bundle `mix` line only on a measured need (D77). An exemplar analysis is the same thing with a MEASURED w.
  Distinct from [FINDINGS-SELINC] (a filter on which kinds a link contributes, not a mixture).

- [STAMP-REVIEW] STATE:not-started — REVIEW THE STAMP CONCEPT
  filed: 2026-10-09 Frank
  status (2026-10-09): not started; FILED 2026-10-09 by Frank, session 102; FOR EVALUATION; pairs with [RXINFO-REMODEL]
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: tests/codegen; decisions D46 D156
  Today every artifact carries ~75 `#define <PREFIX>_*` stamps (69 in the .c, 6 in the .h on `a(b|c)+d`: ENGINE/ENGINE_SEL/ENGINE_WHY, DFA_SCAN/PREFILTER/TABLE/START/..., VM_PREFILTER/RESEED/RUNGS/STRATS/..., REQ_*, END_WINDOW, UNROLL_K, the cap echoes, ...), each written by hand at its emitter site; several have DRIFTED from reality (DFA_START "reverse-pass" on 452 artifacts with no reverse machine; spec's "unanchored" = fwd+rev false on pinned artifacts; END_WINDOW over a bare `return 0`), the same disease as `rx_info.engine`.
  Frank's three questions: (1) DO WE NEED THEM? — name the real use cases (D46 observability: every strategy choice observable; tests/codegen and run_*_stamps checks; emit_sweep/census/mech SAB_REACH reading them as instruments; the pcrec-bench adapter's attribution (closed enums in adapter.py/report.py/selfcheck.py); compile-time-visible facts a caller's `#if` could use (engine_m4.md §5.5's reason for keeping macros beside rx_info); a human reading the artifact) and which are real vs incidental; (2) COULD rx_info ALONE serve? — what needs compile-time visibility (any `#if` consumer in or outside the tree?) vs link/run-time; whether tests and the bench can read rx_info (or an `--emit-facts`-style listing) instead of grepping the C; cost (rx_info is in the binary, stamps are free); (3) IF KEPT, a better organization? — generated from one source (D156's derived path; locate_finish.md rev 2.1's generated off-path rule; the [RXINFO-REMODEL] sections), one family per section, a closed
  vocabulary listed by the compiler (`--list-stamps`?), stamp lines not counted by the size caps ([CAP-STAMP-BYTES]). DELIVERABLE when opened: a survey of every stamp's readers by grep (in-tree + pcrec-bench), a use-case table, and a recommendation; sequenced WITH [RXINFO-REMODEL]. CONFIRMED (Frank's Q3 on locate × finish, D156 addendum 2, 2026-10-09): the generated off-path rule applies to the stamps as they stand.
- [EFFORT-BUDGET] STATE:not-started — ONE EFFORT BUDGET instead of two or three
  filed: 2026-10-09 Frank
  status (2026-10-11): SHARED VOCABULARY, Frank 2026-10-11 session 108: the per-piece effort weights read [PRIOR-COST-UNIFY]'s operation-kind table — the CONTRACT-marked column — rather than a second inventory of what an operation is. FILED 2026-10-09 by Frank, session 103; FOR EVALUATION, unscheduled; owns K102
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: decisions D6 D49 D51 D158; known_issues K102
  Frank: "even outside this issue, a single budget might be a better usability choice. explaining the 2-3 budgets someone has to dial in and why is tricky." His sketch: an abstract EFFORT counter that every piece of matcher work charges, WEIGHTED by what the piece is, with the WEIGHTS as the dials, "not steps or whatever". Evidence that started it: K102's witness (`\b(\w+)\b\s+\1\b`, 809-byte subject, measured session 103). Possessify off needs 800 steps + 394 work = 1,194. Possessify on needs 0 + 1,199. The effort is conserved, and the optimization only RE-CATEGORIZES it from step to work, which breaks D158 for any caller who tunes one counter. Today's categories are MECHANISMS, not costs: STEP = backtrack resumptions with forward progress free (D51); WORK = forward-only work the fail label never sees (D49, added to bound the possessify quadratic).
  FIRST STEP when opened: a STUDY lane (opus) games it out before any design.
  It answers: (1) the unit and the per-piece weights (byte examined, backtrack resume, frame push/discard, scan iteration, literal-run compare, trie byte), and whether forward progress, free today, gets charged; (2) whether the weights are caller dials, compiler-derived, or a fixed table ([OPT-DIAL] relation; memory pcrec-suspect-tuning-constants: measure or derive, never guess); (3) D158 by construction: every optimization's charge ≤ its denied build's, provable per optimization (witness: explain K102's +5 residue); (4) which bounds stay SEPARATE: frames/trail/recurse are MEMORY bounds, not effort (sibling-of-a-family lens over limits.md's whole budget family); (5) caller usability, meaning one number a user can reason about, its default calibration (D51's ~10 s honest-refusal posture) and how its spec reads; (6) the surface cost: `pcrec_options`, CLI flags, `rx_info`/stamps, limits.md/tuning.md, GIVEUP1/[GIVEUP-DIFF], bench adapter readers (census by grep, never a hand list); (7) the
  run-time cost of a weighted counter in the emitted hot loops. A design note + D6 panel follow the study. Interim: D158 holds with K102 as its ONE accepted exception (D158 addendum 1).
- [RXINFO-REMODEL] STATE:not-started — a deliberate remodel of rx_info
  filed: 2026-10-09 Frank
  status (2026-10-09): not started; FILED 2026-10-09 by Frank, session 102, from reading docs/spec/match_api.md; FOR EVALUATION, unscheduled
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: docs/spec/match_api.md; docs/design/locate_finish.md; decisions D80 D81 D156
  A DELIBERATE REMODEL OF `rx_info`, which has grown incrementally and shows it (today a flat list: abi, flags, encoding, ncaps/ngroups/nnames, engine, five budgets/capacities, two frame sizes, pattern/pattern_len, groups, engine_why, scan, search_form, name, nentries, ... each field added by the row that needed it).
  Frank's two points: (1) `rx_info.engine` (DFA/VM) no longer describes what an artifact does; it should reflect the search's SECTIONS as D156's locate × finish design names them (pre-check, locator/finder, finisher, ...), i.e. the selected path (docs/design/locate_finish.md rev 2.1's derived path/needs is the natural source); (2) organize `rx_info` into SECTIONS as SUB-STRUCTS (identity / pattern; options and encoding; captures and groups; search path: precheck, locate, finish; runtime bounds; entries) so a consumer can reference just one section, and TIE IT TO [V-E]'s searchable array of rx_info (the organizer: find an artifact by any field).
  EVAL QUESTIONS: how sections relate to the RX_* stamp families (one section per stamp family, the macros a projection of it?); `abi` and `name` stay first so any reader can identify the layout; whether `engine` survives as a derived convenience; the pcrec-bench shim (~40 `pb_*` accessors over rx_info) as the first consumer to migrate (an [inbox] note + window handshake); pre-1.0 ABI change is allowed but deliberate (memory: abi changes pre-release = ritual, not avoidance; D81's abi reset to 1 at 1.0 is a natural landing window). (3, Frank 2026-10-09) EVERY FIELD const-qualified at the MEMBER level, sub-structs included, pointer fields `T *const` (e.g. `const char *const pattern`), not only the `extern const rx_info <prefix>_info` object, so a caller's copy is immutable too and the type itself documents the contract.

- [PAT-LINT] STATE:not-started — OPTIONAL PATTERN ANALYSIS
  filed: 2026-08-25 Frank
  status (2026-08-25): not started
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: decisions D38 D74; known_issues K34
  an ahead-of-time check that names issues in a pattern the engines will accept but that behave badly (Frank, 2026-08-25, fortieth session, from the K34 ruling D74; PARKED — no queue position; details and design TBD). The charter case: ZERO-PROGRESS LEFT RECURSION — `(a|(?1)a)b` re-enters group 1 at the same subject position before consuming, so every non-matching subject is a runaway (pcrec gives up `frames`, PCRE2 concludes only by an implementation artefact, D74); the same language as `(a(?1)?)b` or `a+b`, which recurse after consuming and conclude in both engines. Rule 1: a call edge to group G reachable from G's own entry through a ZERO-MINIMUM-WIDTH prefix (the compiler already computes minimum widths — wave E's root-minw guard — so the analysis is a walk over facts it has).
  WARNING, NEVER A REFUSAL: the pattern is legal and matches on positive subjects; §3.3's deliberate same-position recursion with a base case is not a defect (the analysis says "may not terminate on non-matching input", not "wrong"). Other candidates for the same surface, each to be measured for a real occurrence before it is built: nested unbounded quantifiers over overlapping languages (the classic `(a+)+b` catastrophic-backtracking shape — relevant only where pcrec picks the VM engine; the DFA is immune, so the analysis must report ENGINE-AWARE, else it warns about a pattern that runs in linear time); empty-language callees (already refused at wave E — a diagnostic tier question whether it moves here); a dead group that never captures (wave G's elision list — informational).
  SURFACE TBD: a CLI flag (`--analyze` / `-W`) whose output is text, or a library entry; the D38 naming rules apply to any stamped result. Interacts with [V-G] (user-facing regex testing — the natural home if that lands first) and [V-H] (trace modes show the runaway; this names it before running). NOT QUEUED — parks behind the general work per the boonies discipline.

Frank: "for simd and arch there will be sets of choices that should be toggled together — `simd`, `no-simd` and the various arch sets. They overlap. Plus the dial work is effectively 5 sets of options. Formalize the idea of option sets and how they interact; there may be other logical sets." Today's pieces this must unify rather than add beside (general-mechanisms rule): the per-optimization deny/force flags (`-fno-*`, deny bits through 46, `--list-axes`), `--tune=N`'s five pinned profiles (docs/spec/tuning.md §5, D103), `--engine`, encodings, `--features` modules, findings bundles, and the coming [MEMFN] families (ISA level v1/v3/v4 / armv8, SIMD on/off, the `--memfn-deny=` family proposal, isa_selection.md/isa_evaluation.md/integration.md).
  Design questions: what a set IS (a named bundle of option assignments vs a predicate over the option space); membership and OVERLAP (a flag in several sets); composition and precedence (set vs set, set vs explicit flag, file-wins, `.rxt` config blocks); conflicts refused by name, never resolved silently; how a set is STAMPED in the artifact and in rx_info; how test-axes, the identity gates and the bench treat a set as an axis; and whether the dial's five positions become five sets. First step (DONE: design note option_sets.md, then the light panel r1); no build until a consumer ([MEMFN] R4's ISA/SIMD rows) needs it (D77).

- [MATCH-EVENTS] STATE:not-started — a callback per matched piece, distinct from a callout
  filed: 2026-10-12 Frank
  status (2026-10-12): not started; FILED 2026-10-12 by Frank, session 110: "output pieces as they're matched like a rx pattern that matches json. A callout placed after a piece gets matched is effectively a callback for the pieces of the json"; filed, NOT scheduled; D163 add. 6
  trigger: a user ask for structured extraction (JSON/log field streaming), or module `callouts` building (design both together so the binding surface is one).
  refs: decisions D163
  (a callout decides; an event only reports). Callouts cannot serve it: they fire on tentative paths that backtracking abandons and at an engine-relative set of positions (the purity contract, D163 add. 6 Q2). Shape (lean): an event marker in the pattern records (event id, position) on the match path the way captures do — TRAILED, so backtracking undoes it — and the events of the FINAL match are delivered in order to a caller callback after it commits; repeated groups deliver every iteration (which captures cannot: they keep the last). Streaming before the end is possible only at points the match can no longer retreat past (an atomic group / cut / `(*COMMIT)`), where buffered events can be flushed early. Engine: the VM (it has the trail); a DFA-routed pattern would need a DFA locate + VM replay over the span (compound client 2's shape).
  Composes with [M4-SUBST]'s renderer and the [VAR] value model. TRIGGER: a user ask for structured extraction (JSON/log field streaming), or module `callouts` building (design both together so the binding surface is one).

  Given the SETS, drive the engine generator once per point of the product and emit the selector over the results. Its set-valued request surface lives HERE, not in `pcrec_options` — D20 deletes the API-change half of this step, because a generator that only ever compiles one point is correctly served by scalars. Two properties from D18 to preserve and to test structurally: dispatch resolves ONCE per search call and never reaches the hot loop, and a request with no plural dimension emits byte-for-byte what pcrec emits today (no dispatcher, no extra parameter, no indirection). Emit named per-combination entry points (`rx_search_ci_utf8`) as well as the selector, so a statically-known caller pays no dispatch at all.
  DEFERRED BY DESIGN: build this only once a dimension has actually survived D18's earn-its-axis test with a measurement behind it — if the OS-1/OS-2 predictions hold, it has no customer yet, and that is a good outcome rather than a stalled one RULE FOR THE WHOLE OS FAMILY (D18, moved here from the retired section intro 2026-10-12): the caller names a SET of values per dimension and the product is over those sets; a singleton set is fully specialized and compiled away (asking for case-insensitive ONLY is hyperspecialization, not an axis), so a dimension is an axis only when its set has 2+ elements; before it becomes an axis, measure whether specializing buys anything — a dimension that folds into the front end, is free at run time, or is a pure wrapper is NOT an axis even when the caller names it plural; record the measurement against D18's prediction, including when the prediction was wrong.
- [SR-5] STATE:not-started — guard the fast-path claim, do not just assert it
  filed: 2026-08-13 (first appearance in plan.md, git; author unrecorded)
  status (2026-08-13): not started
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  guard the fast path CLAIM, do not just assert it. REWRITE THE ASSERTION FIRST (R6): the claim as written here — "base-tier patterns must perform ZERO registry lookups (`(?:` excepted)" — is FALSE in both directions, measured with an instrumented build on 2026-08-10. `(?:` performs zero (parse.c answers it before the registry), while `[abc]` performs one and `[a-z]+@[a-z]+\.[a-z]{2,4}` performs three, because the class-bracket doorway is on the base-tier path. Written as specified, SR-5 would fail the moment it was built, or would have to assert something untrue. The property actually worth guarding is a BOUND: lookups <= one per non-negated `[` plus one per `[` inside a class, and zero for a pattern with no character class.
  Use an instrumented build with a lookup counter, the way run_trie_identity.sh uses `-DPCREC_NO_TRIE`, so no counter exists in the shipped build (TS-1 would reject one anyway). Pair with the M2.9 compile-time budgets

Per-artifact: the entries, the `<p>_info` instance, `<p>_buffers`, the `<P>_NCAPS`/frame macros, the outer guard.
  THE WORK: an abi-VERSIONED guard (different-abi artifacts in one TU fail loudly instead of silently sharing the first block), a byte-identity PIN on the block across artifacts, an optional standalone export of the block, and the fixed spelling for the .c's internal statics/labels/macros.
- [UCD-RECORD] STATE:not-started — ONE SHARED PER-CODE-POINT PROPERTY TABLE
  filed: 2026-09-28 Frank
  status (2026-09-28): not started; FILED by Frank 2026-09-28, eighty-fourth session, D125: filed, UNSCHEDULED; decide by research + tests, like [UCP]
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: decisions D125; known_issues K70
  a lookup that returns a RECORD for a code point — its case-fold data (other case / case set) and a BITMAP of the standard classes it belongs to (general category, \w/\d/\s under UCP, POSIX classes, script if wanted). LINKED ONCE ([XART-TABLES] is its delivery mechanism), and an AMORTIZED cost shared by many UTF features at once: UCP \w/\d/\s/\b, \p{..}, caseless folding, (?r) restrict ([K70]'s priced implementation needs a third fold spelling — a record table could be that one spelling). PRECEDENT: PCRE2's own ucd.c is exactly this shape (a two-stage table -> ucd_record {chartype, script, caseset, other_case, bprops...}); ICU's UTrie2 likewise. RELATION TO [CLS-TREE]: complementary, not competing — CLS-TREE composes a SPECIALIZED matcher per class (pcrec's AOT spirit; a small class like \d is a few compares); a record table is a GENERAL probe (two loads + a bit test for ANY class).
  A natural shape: the record probe becomes one more [CLS-TREE] KIT MEMBER, chosen by the dial when a specialized form would be larger than the shared probe, or when a pattern uses many Unicode classes at once. SCOPE NOTE: the byte-level DFA transitions stay byte automata; a record table serves the VM, inserted checks / position views over decoded characters ([UCP] idea 1), and compile time. MEASUREMENTS a study owes: the table's size for Unicode 16 (distinct records, stage sizes) from third_party/ucd-16.0.0; the probe's per-character cost vs CLS-TREE kit forms on the utf8 bench; how many shipped/bench patterns use ≥2 Unicode classes (where sharing pays).

  Lane tri86 built HALF of this (`0058b93c`): under `--dump` only, `run.sh`'s P-C2 floor ("no pattern blocks parsed from file") is now DECLARATION-GATED — a head-bearing file whose own head (`pcrec --list-source`) declares zero `pattern` rows is not scored as a corpus file that forgot its cases.
  The OTHER half is still a PATH-SHAPED STOPGAP: `tests/findings/adversarial/`, `tests/findings/witness/` and `tests/findings/golden/` are excluded from `run.sh`'s default (no-argument) sweep by `-not -path`, alongside `tests/known_fail/` — because five of golden's eight files use an analyzer-output dialect (`pcrec-analyze`'s own "bigram" bundle scan-kind, a looser provenance rule) that `pcrec --list-source` cannot parse AT ALL today, so the declaration gate never gets a chance to run on them; something else (a real parse failure) fires first, correctly, but only because the path exclusion catches the population first.
  REMAINING WORK: teach `pcrec --list-source` the analyzer/pcrec dialect gap (golden's "bigram" directive, its looser provenance rule) so ALL bundle files — declared, not merely path-matched — are recognised the same way under an ORDINARY run, not only `--dump`; then the three `-not -path` exclusions collapse to the one general declaration mechanism. Explicitly out of scope for both tri86 (whose own charter named it "not this triage's job") and findb2tri (whose fix was itself the interim path-shaped stopgap). No trigger stated; D125 phase-4 stock-take decides sequencing.
- [LIST-CLASSES] STATE:not-started — A `--list-classes` DEBUGGING LISTING of every named class and its composition
  filed: 2026-09-28 Frank
  status (2026-09-28): not started; FILED 2026-09-28 by Frank, eighty-fifth session, UNSCHEDULED: "File an item to --list the class names/compositions. Remember that there may be more than the two encodes. Therefore encode is a column. Can be long and exhaustive- it's a debugging tool"
  next: starts on Frank's word; no row-specific trigger recorded
  refs: docs/spec/cli.md; decisions D80 D130
  One row per (class spelling × ENCODING × UCP/restriction configuration). ENCODING IS A COLUMN, never a per-encoding section or flag: encodings beyond byte/utf8 are expected ([UTF-RW], latin1, …), so the listing iterates the encoding registry and never names the encodings it knows. Rows cover every escape (\d \s \w \h \v \R …, negations), every POSIX class, every \p{…} property/script/general category, the UCP (D130) and (?a…)-restricted variants, and the caseless-folded forms. Columns: spelling, encoding, config (UCP, restriction knob, caseless), the resolved composition (the interval set in code points, plus the definitions-table/DEF_UCP row that produced it and its spelled composition, e.g. how [:graph:] is built), interval count, member count, and optionally the form the [CLS-TREE] kit/dial picks per --tune position.
  ALIASES (Frank 2026-09-28, "there are names like Cyrillic"): every accepted spelling of a \p set is its own row — script names (\p{Cyrillic}), Script=/sc= long and short forms (\p{sc=Cyrl}), script extensions (\p{scx:Cyrillic}), category long/short names — with SPELLING and CANONICAL SET as separate columns, so aliases that should agree visibly resolve to one set. Recognised-but-unimplemented properties (probe 2026-09-28: \p{Bidi_Class=L} → "not implemented yet") are rows too, marked unimplemented — the listing shows the gaps as well as the coverage. Exhaustive and long by design (thousands of rows — a debugging tool, not a user summary), filterable by a spelling/encoding argument. Complements --list-definitions (the rows) and --list-syntax (the spellings); part of the [LIST-TABLES] family. Spec hunk (D80) in docs/spec/cli.md when built.
  Natural home: after [UCP] U1 lands (the configurations exist then).

  every `make test` / `make asan` / `make ubsan` / `make test-axes` run appends one row per section (and, for the axes sweep, one row per axis) to a committed `docs/dev/test_runtime_log.tsv`: date, box (`uname` + a hostname class, since "the Mac" vs "ubuntubudu" is the distinction every entry in docs/testing.md's 2026-09-25/26 "Suite health log" entry needed and didn't have machine-readably), tree SHA + dirty bit, section/axis name, wall seconds, verdict, and the `PROCS`/`HARNESS_BATCH` values in effect — written by the existing completion trailer (`tests/lib/test_trailer.sh` already carries `RUN_STAMP_*` and the trailer directory; this row extends it rather than adding a second mechanism).
  `docs/dev/artifact_size_log.tsv` is ALREADY exactly this shape of log (one row per run, committed, diffable) but lanes/the manager have been treating its per-run growth as noise and discarding it with `git checkout --` before commit — this row's own design must decide whether `test_runtime_log.tsv`'s appends are committed periodically (e.g. at merge, the artifact-size log's own intended discipline per docs/testing.md "The artifact-size log") rather than repeating that discard. Timeouts (this file's own `RE-RECORD TRIGGER`, `K7_CPU`-family budgets, [TEST-REENTRANT]'s per-unit markers below) get sized from this log's real recent numbers instead of a number pinned once and read stale months later (docs/testing.md's 2026-09-25/26 entry catches two such stale reads).
  D77: no build before the design question above is answered — whether to widen `test_trailer.sh` in place or add a sibling writer. UNSCHEDULED (Frank 2026-09-26: "don't schedule yet. Let's close tickets before we open more") — no lane until the open queue closes, on his word.
  AMENDED (Frank 2026-09-26): (1) record TEST COUNTS per section/axis, not just time — tests run, passed, failed, skipped, info, allowed give-ups, % passing, and derived s/test — so time vs #tests can be read as linear or not (scrape the sections' existing `checks passed:`/`checks failed:`/case counts in the trailer; a section with no count is recorded UNCOUNTED, never 0); (2) record the ARCH and environment every row ran under: arch (arm64/x86_64), OS, hostname class, gcc version, repo/remote; (3) MERGEABLE across boxes/repos/branches: ONE FILE PER RUN under docs/dev/test_metrics/ (<date>_<host>_<arch>_<sha>_<runid>.tsv), rows uniquely keyed (run id + section) so any union is a valid dataset and duplicates dedupe; a script builds the trend view on demand (nothing aggregated is committed); chosen over a single file + .gitattributes merge=union (tool/repo-dependent).
  This SETTLES the row's open question: artifact_size_log.tsv moves to the same per-run scheme and stops being discarded.

- [REPEAT-RUN] STATE:not-started — required-run and span analysis should see through exact repeats (`A{4}` reads like `AAAA`)
  filed: 2026-10-13 Frank (session 111), "file it for later review"
  status (2026-10-13): not started; probe only (abi 76): `AAAA` and `A{4}` give identical DFA tables, but only `AAAA` gets REQ_RUN "41414141@3" (memchr+memcmp start, DFA handoff) and the VM literal compare; `A{4}` gets REQ_RUN "none", a plain memchr start and a counter-rung span loop (VM program 257 -> 662 bytes)
  next: Frank's review; no measurement lane until then
  refs: memfn/docs/ideas.md (2026-10-13 entry); [SPAN-ISEL]
  The general form: an exact repeat `X{n}` of a fixed-width atom IS a width-n*w member, so the run extractor (and the VM's literal-run selection) should read it as such (`ab{3}c` has required run `abbbc`; `[0-9]{4}` is a width-4 span member). Fix the reading in the analysis, never a parser rewrite to a sequence. Likely cost: the VM half (counter loop vs one memcmp) more than the search-start half. Population unmeasured.
  Frank, 2026-10-13: "pcrec could construct a span for memfn that isn't actually what the pattern says." GENERAL FORM: a span is a fact about the LANGUAGE, derived from IR/automaton, not the spelling (`A{4}`->`AAAA`, `abc|abd`->`ab[cd]`, `a(?=bc)`->`abc` as a search condition). Soundness bar by use: a SEARCH span need only be necessary (every match has it); a MATCH-STEP span must be exact. Facts only to the kit (D157); each derivation needs an identity proof + a planted-wrong-derivation sabotage.

- [FIND-BOUNDARY-SIDE] STATE:not-started — a find site may scan for the boundary's other side (`[a-z]+\b`: scan for \W, not [a-z])
  filed: 2026-10-13 Frank via the kit (session 111), on R17-b's ALPHA losses: "not your issue" (the kit's): pcrec's choice of what the FIND site scans for
  status (2026-10-13): F2 (kit, responses.md 56f8c37a0): bounded prose misses ~145 branches/KiB under EVERY scan form, so the loss is the consumer's per-match verify, not the scan or origin; candidate levers: batching (next_n), [REPEAT-RUN]
  next: discuss with Frank (D161 window/landmark territory) before any design lane
  refs: decisions D161; docs/design/memfn/window_contract.md; memfn/docs/ideas.md; [REPEAT-RUN]; [START-DENSE]
  The general form: a match is bounded by conditions on both sides; the find site should scan for the RAREST necessary condition, which can be the transition OUT of the run (the \b side, \W) rather than the run's own set. pcrec owns the choice of origin (D161 add. 9); the kit owns the method.

- [BREF-SPAN] STATE:not-started — a fixed-offset backreference as a superset DFA plus one span-equality check
  filed: 2026-10-13 Frank (session 111), "yes file it"
  status (2026-10-13): not started; `([abc]{4})blah\1` today: ENGINE "vm" (capture group), REQ_RUN "626c6168@0"
  next: Frank's review; no measurement lane until then
  refs: [REPEAT-RUN]; [BREF-FOLD-ONCE]; [M4-CALLOUTS]; [POSTFACT]
  Replace `\1` by its group's LANGUAGE (a necessary superset the DFA runs at full speed), then verify the two fixed-offset spans are equal (one memcmp; folded for caseless). A pure DFA is possible (finite capture set) but k^w states. Admission: group and `\1` at fixed offsets from the match start (or both from the end), the group captured once; variable widths or re-captures decline (a failed equality could hide another decomposition at the same start). The group span is known, so captures come free.

- [POSTFACT] STATE:not-started — user-supplied predicates on captures ("post facts", e.g. `int($1)<=255`), compiled into the matcher
  filed: 2026-10-13 Frank (session 111)
  status (2026-10-13): not started; idea stage, discuss before any design lane
  next: discussion with Frank: syntax/module (a non-PCRE extension, D26), placement semantics, the three tiers below
  refs: [M4-CALLOUTS]; [VAR]; [BREF-SPAN]; decisions D26; docs/design/plan_row_bodies/POSTFACT.md
  Frank: callouts can express it (by design), but lean into compilation: place the check INLINE, and check each \d as it arrives for a quicker bail. Three tiers by what the predicate is: (1) REGULAR predicates (numeric range over a digit span, length, set membership) LOWER INTO THE AUTOMATON (pcrec generates the range regex), so the DFA keeps its speed and bails per digit for free; (2) non-regular predicates as compiled inline assertions at their placement point (VM; callout semantics: fail = backtrack); (3) DFA candidate + post-verify only where the decomposition is unique (fixed widths, as [BREF-SPAN]).
  HOW GENERAL (2026-10-13): one-integer linear predicates (ranges, mod m, and/or/not) are ALWAYS regular over decimal digits (Presburger / Buchi-Bruyere) with small DFAs; relations between two sequential numbers (`$1<$2`) or length-vs-value counters are regular ONLY when bounded, with states ~ product of value ranges. Rule: lower when one-variable, or bounded and the priced state count fits the size ladder; else lower a NECESSARY approximation (digit count, leading digit) and check the rest inline.

- [CAPTURE-ARRAY] STATE:not-started — a repeated capture group records EVERY iteration (an array), not only the last
  filed: 2026-10-13 Frank (session 111), witness `(?:(\d+)\s*\,?\s*){1,50}`
  status (2026-10-13): not started; idea stage; PCRE2 keeps only the last iteration (a pcrec extension, D26)
  next: discussion with Frank (opt-in syntax per group, API shape, oracle)
  refs: [POSTFACT]; [VAR]; [M4-CALLOUTS]; decisions D26; docs/spec/match_api.md (rx_group_entry)
  Prior art: .NET Group.Captures, Python `regex` captures(). Opt-in PER GROUP so other groups pay nothing. Bounded repeats ({1,50}) give a fixed slot array; unbounded needs a caller buffer plus an overflow code. VM: slot i written by iteration i, the count trailed so backtracking discards abandoned iterations. DFA: only via tagged-DFA positions per iteration (bounded), likely VM-only first. An API addition and abi event; build it BEFORE [IFACE-PUSH] (Frank 2026-10-13: features first, so the push knows what the new API must hold). Oracle: python `regex` captures() is a new dependency (bench-side or opt-in).

- [DEBUG-CHECKS] STATE:not-started — an opt-in CHECKED generation mode: caller-misuse and invariant checks for development
  filed: 2026-10-16 Frank (session 113, from D165 addendum 3: the iterator owner check dropped from the hot path)
  status (2026-10-16): not started; filed, not scheduled. Charter = inventory first: every dropped or declined guard plus every UB clause the spec states, each as a candidate row
  next: STEP 0 inventory lane (read-only) on Frank's word: list candidate checks with site, cost class and the spec UB clause each would detect
  refs: decisions D165 add. 3, D133 (-futf-check), D45, D158; docs/spec/match_api.md §5.3, §10; findall_iter.md rev 3 (IT4, IT5)
  One opt-in generation axis (a compile-time contract value; never default, never in timing cells) that emits checks for what the release artifact leaves undefined: the iterator's owner/foreign-buffer compare (dropped by D165 add. 3), subject/vars/buffer immutability while an iterator is BOUND (a captured hash or length+pointer compare), descriptor sanity (frame/trail sizes vs the artifact's stamps), startpos/caps/NULL arguments, buffer-overrun canaries on caller-provided frames, and internal invariant asserts (cursor monotonicity, LOWER-handoff progress, MRL bound).
  Questions for its design: one axis or per-check denies (the [OPT-SETS] set shape: `debug` as a named set over check rows); the report channel (a distinct give-up code vs __builtin_trap); whether `-futf-check` and `-fstartpos-guard` fold in as members; how test-axes and the sanitizer battery use it (a natural pairing with make asan/ubsan); the abi/stamp rule (a CHECKED stamp so a checked artifact is never benchmarked by mistake).
- [DECISION-LOG] STATE:not-started — an opt-in DECISION CHANNEL: every compile-time choice, what went into it, and why
  filed: 2026-10-16 Frank (session 113: "as pcrec, or perhaps even memfn, makes choices about which way to go, it outputs text to this channel")
  status (2026-10-16): not started; filed, not scheduled. Absorbs the analysis-bundle demotion report (D162 add. 4 gap) and the part-layout/rank choices (D163 add. 9)
  next: design note on Frank's word; STEP 0 = inventory of decision sites by the selection tables (cand_rows, fit_rungs, entry_rows/part-layout, req_admits, dfa_pfs, kit fd_rows)
  refs: decisions D162 add. 4, D163 add. 8-9, D157; rows [LIST-TABLES], [V-H], [DEBUG-CHECKS], [KNOW-SRC]; start_table.md C0's selection trace; findings.md §7
  One channel, off by default and switchable (a CLI flag / pcrec_options field / config line; never changes an artifact byte), on which every decision site writes one record: the table and the row that fired, the rows that declined ahead of it and the conjunct that failed, the inputs read (facts, analysis block + provenance + why, measurement-set row, explicit control vs preset), and a held/declined outcome where one applies. pcrec's tables write pcrec records; the kit writes its OWN records through a text callback on the seam (facts in, kit text out: D157 holds).
  Family (SIBLING lens): the compile-time selection trace start_table.md C0 built for the gates is this channel's machine form, so promote it rather than add a parallel writer; [LIST-TABLES] lists what the rows ARE, this lists which fired; `--emit-facts` lists facts. Design question: one channel with a machine (TSV) and a human rendering, or two. Distinct from [V-H] (a runtime matcher trace) and [DEBUG-CHECKS] (runtime checks).
## Boonies
- [ONCE-DEADCHOICE] STATE:not-started — ACCEPT MORE `once` CALLOUT SITES BY SEALING PROVABLY DEAD CHOICES
  filed: 2026-10-26 manager; Frank: "Save your ideas in a boonies plan item to be revisited if requested"
  status (2026-10-26): BOONIES; DECLINED for now (Frank: "both of those options are not easily predictable. It's hard to program around 'maybe it will work'"); `once` stays a syntactic reading rule (INLINE-C.md ruling 2026-10-26). Nothing built.
  trigger: Frank asks for it; or user evidence that the reading rule refuses `once` sites people need, where the fix (possessive / (?>...) / (?=...)) is not acceptable.
  refs: docs/design/plan_row_bodies/INLINE-C.md (ruling 2026-10-26); src/opt/callout.c co_choice (the reading rule); docs/dev/reviews/2026-10-26-r2-ic1b-code.md EMIT-1 (sites are possessify barriers)
  Ideas: (a) DEAD CHOICES: a choice before a site is dead when no retry can change anything observable. Proofs pcrec has or nearly has: possessify's disjointness verdict (every retry fails, e.g. `a+` before `b`); a non-atomic lookaround whose body sets no capture read later (every retry reaches the site in an identical state, e.g. `(?*a+)(?C"x,once")a+c`). Accept `once` when every live choice is dead, and SEAL them (insert the atomic group) as a requirement of the site, not an optimization, so the class stays flag-independent; seal whenever the proof holds so the reported class equals behaviour. (b) Possessify may look PAST a non-consuming site (same position after it); a consume site stays a barrier. (c) The rejected runtime form: skip a re-fire when the site's observable state (position, captures) is unchanged within an attempt. Why declined: acceptance would depend on an optimizer proof the user cannot see when reading the pattern.
- [MEMFN-SKIPSWITCH] STATE:not-started — A WALK <-> WIDE-MASK SWITCH INSIDE THE DFA SKIP LOOP
  filed: 2026-10-21 manager, from the kit's notice of Frank's D169 addendum ruling ("use the walk when the subject is not known")
  status (2026-10-21): BOONIES (Frank: "adaptive methods haven't proved their worth"). Near-duplicate of the kit's adatwin (per-stretch switch -2.2%, reading cost 5-7 ns). Nothing built.
  trigger: bench or user evidence of dense-and-sparse mixed workloads where the walk leaves a measured gain on the table.
  refs: docs/dev/decisions.md D169 addendum; memfn/docs/responses.md (kit notice 2026-10-21, lane/memfn-r27 cf5909eb1); docs/design/memfn/walk_vs_v1_record.md (the closed record: what not to retry, when to re-open)
  Charter: a set-FIND site switches between the walk and a wide-mask form at run time inside pcrec's DFA skip loop, so a subject's sparse stretches get the mask's gain and its dense stretches keep the walk. Note: the kit's adatwin found a per-stretch adaptive PRODUCER does not pay; this row is the switch at pcrec's skip-loop level, a different site.

  filed: 2026-08-29 Frank
  status (2026-08-29): not started; BOONIES TIER — LATE GAME; Frank, 2026-08-29 forty-fifth session, filed UNPLANNED "(ironic)": a doc plan item for a spec on UPGRADING pcrec
  trigger: after [DD-11] and [DD-13b]'s parsers land and the registry surfaces stop growing, or at the 1.0 release-doc pass, whichever is first.

  the recipe-per-extension-kind contract for anyone who grows the compiler AFTER 1.0 — how to add a [DD-11] replacement/definition row (predicate + core-syntax definition; the per-row checks and sabotage rows that must land with it; `--list-definitions` shows it), a registry `RegRow` (D24; the four/five registry surfaces it must appear on and the checks that prove it — PC-3, `--list-syntax`, the reject table), a feature MODULE (parser hook + lowering + tests/<module>/ + "requires module" refusal + compliance-refresh), an emitter FORM / representation object ([ENG-FORM]/D82: one object + one accessor block, its stamp, its deny/force axis row, the form-census floor, the `make test-axes` identity sweep), a STAMP (D81 naming, one derivation two readers, the ABSENCE-as-discriminator hazard), a LIMIT (limits.md's cited-and-re-measured rule, raise-only overrides per D84), an .rxt block kind ([DD-13b]'s grammar), and the ABI ritual (D76's four sites) — each recipe naming the checks that must
  move in the same change and the spec file that must gain its hunk (D80). It is a SPEC (contract) tier document per D80, not a guide: it says what an upgrade MUST do to be accepted, and points at the design notes for why. WHY LATE: every recipe is a restatement of a mechanism that is still moving ([DD-11]'s table, [DD-13b]'s grammar, the abi resetting to 1 at 1.0); written earlier it drifts with each lane (learnings §3's "one derivation" applies to prose too — the recipes should be written against the STABLE surfaces and, where a surface is listable, say "run `--list-X`" instead of restating the table). Trigger: after [DD-11] and [DD-13b]'s parsers land and the registry surfaces stop growing, or at the 1.0 release-doc pass, whichever is first. Starts only on Frank's word.
- [SL-COMPILE-TIME] STATE:not-started — START-LANDING SL1 walks cost compile time on very wide start frontiers
  filed: 2026-10-11 filer not recorded
  status (2026-10-11): not started; BOONIES; FILED 2026-10-11, session 107, from bench O-97 ask 2
  trigger: a consumer for whom pcrec compile time matters, or a pattern where the walk dominates.
  refs: decisions D45
  [START-LANDING] SL1's `land_char`/`width` walks cost compile time on a very wide start frontier: bench emit-c x2.53 on wild-datetime-datefinder-alternation (173 -> 437 ms). Manager paired compile on the dev box (loaded box, 3 interleaved pairs, pattern text from the bench's members.tsv): default 222-224 ms vs `-fno-start-landing -fno-start-width` 124-126 ms, both on the SIZE-REFUSAL path (589,053 B > 500,000 at default flags) — so the walks also run for a compile that is then refused. Far under D45; gcc phase unchanged. TRIGGER: a consumer for whom pcrec compile time matters, or a pattern where the walk dominates. Cheap ideas when triggered: run the analysis after the size checks; memoize `land_char` per closure.

Scope question on file: does a kinds filter apply to one link or the whole onward chain (fcrit-model finding 9). Trigger: a real user bundle that needs two sources for disjoint kinds.

  Design points to settle at charter: NAME RESOLUTION (pattern's own groups first, then included libraries in order; collisions refused by name; PCRE2 has no include, so this is pcrec's compile-time namespace — the oracle sees the EXPANDED pattern, which makes the harness's expansion the SPLICE-vs-LINKAGE `A == B` control by construction); the .rxt directives (`define name <pattern>` / `lib <file>` per file or block; the harness expands for libpcre2 and passes the library to pcrec); the API surface (`pcrec_options` gains a definitions input; CLI `--lib FILE`; versioning per [DD-3]); the STORE's discipline (each entry oracle-verified, a D27-blinded author writes its corpus, pcrec-bench carries it as a benchmark row; the RFC 5322 email specimen is entry #1). Depends on [DD-14] closing (G in particular).

Hazard: static-catalog variants must NOT carry GNU_PROPERTY_X86_ISA_1_NEEDED (OR-merged across the link). Depends on S1 (rx_entries) and [MEMFN]'s A3 stamp; K79/K80 discharged.   (Frank, 2026-08-18, thirty-third session; LOW PRIORITY, boonies tier; details and design TBD): an emitted per-pattern helper that takes a search's results — in particular a captures-delivering search, most particularly one with NAMED groups — and copies them into a STATIC STRUCTURE keyed by the group names (the natural shape: an emitted `struct <prefix>_groups` with one span member per named group, plus a copier from the caps array).
  Generation-time is what makes it cheap: pcrec knows every name at emit time, and [M6.3]'s name grammar ([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,127}) makes every group name a lexically valid C identifier ALMOST for free — the TBD wrinkles already visible: C KEYWORDS are valid group names but invalid member names (`(?<int>...)`, `(?<return>...)` need a mangling rule), and D61's caps-layout promise plus rx_group_entry.slot are the substrate the copier reads through (slot-aware from birth, so a future ref-bearing row costs nothing). Interacts with [V-B] (bindings would love the same struct) and the D38 naming rules. NOT QUEUED — parks behind the general work per the boonies discipline

  module `callouts` (D36: Frank re-scoped `(?C` from NEVER to PLANNED, 2026-08-12 — LOW PRIORITY, deliberately in the queue boonies). Two separable steps: (1) THE FLIP, schedulable any time a lane is free: registry `(?C` row ROADMAP_NEVER → PLANNED, the diagnostic moves from "no module will implement" to "requires module 'callouts'", reject + case11 pins move with it failing-first (NOTE, mod08fix lane 2026-08-12: case11 asserts `(?C1)` `roadmap never`, `names —`, and the "no module will implement it" status — load-bearing against today's tree; they MUST move inside the flip commit or the flip lands red), compliance prose updated IN THE SAME CHANGE (the K14 prose⇔column check binds them), and note the ROADMAP_NEVER live population drops to zero — the never branch stays, column-derived, covered the day a second row exists.
  (2) THE BEHAVIOR, M4-hosted and engine-forcing (VM only — the compiled DFA erases the pattern positions a callout fires at): static extern binding (`extern int rx_callout_n(const rx_callout_block *)` defined by the embedding program — compile-time binding, zero cost when absent; V-A's compat layer later implements pcre2_set_callout as a trampoline ON TOP of this primitive, not instead of it), callback block and return semantics (0/positive/negative) mirroring pcre2_callout_block exactly (D26-exact tier), fire-point discipline DOCUMENTED as engine-relative with PCRE2's own PCRE2_NO_START_OPTIMIZE latitude as the cited precedent.
